# gates-20261122-1551 — gate-watcher lane (lane 5), read-only pass

Run 20261122-1551. Read-only monitor: nothing was pushed, labelled, rerun, merged, replied to, or edited.
Read window: 2026-11-22T15:57:27Z .. 2026-11-22T16:06:55Z. Every number below is from an API call inside that window.
Repo: Zoo-Code-Org/Zoo-Code. upstream/main at read: 410e5ad3d5 (the queue.json scan base was 5ba06451e0 — main moved +7 commits, see MAIN MOVEMENT).

Sources, per PR: GET /pulls/<n>; GET /commits/<head>/check-runs?per_page=100 (latest run per name chosen by max check-run id);
GET /commits/<head>/status (the review gate lives there — see GATE MECHANISM); GET /issues/<n>/comments?since=...;
GET /pulls/<n>/comments (review threads, not /pulls/<n>/review-comments, which 404s);
one GraphQL call with 10 PR aliases for headRefOid / mergeStateStatus / reviewDecision / labels / reviewThreads / reviews(last:8) — cost 6, 4978 left.
Flagged lines were re-verified by pulling the file blob at the head sha (not the bot snippet).

## RULESET (re-read, rulesets/15494382 "Protect Main", enforcement active)
Required status checks, exactly: check-translations, platform-unit-test (ubuntu-latest), platform-unit-test (windows-latest), compile, knip, e2e-mock.
Required reviews: 1 approving + require_code_owner_review true; dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push true; require_review_thread_resolution FALSE;
require_last_push_approval false; require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes true; strict_required_status_checks_policy false (a PR does not need current main).
Merge queue active: SQUASH, grouping ALLGREEN, min-merge-wait 5 min.
Consequence: reviewDecision REVIEW_REQUIRED + mergeable_state blocked = the human/code-owner seat is empty, which is class D and is not the author's to clear.
Advisory only (never in the required list): mutation-diff, codecov/patch, CodeRabbit review state, the Zoo Code / PR review gate status, and the awaiting-* labels.

## GATE MECHANISM — why no head prints a "Zoo Code / PR review gate" check row
The gate is a LEGACY COMMIT STATUS, not a check run: .github/workflows/label-pr-review-state.yml defines reviewGateName at :320 and publishes it with
repos.createCommitStatus (:667-675), reading the previous value back with getCombinedStatusForRef (:648-654).
So /commits/<sha>/check-runs can never show it and a MISSING row there is not a missing gate; it was read from /commits/<sha>/status for all ten heads.
The reconcile pair ("Zoo Code / resolve PRs to reconcile", "Zoo Code / reconcile PR review state (<n>)") is a different, ordinary check pair and is success everywhere below.
The combined-status object was used only to locate the gate row, never as the CI verdict (zoo-pr-blocker §2.3).

## MAIN MOVEMENT
compare 5ba06451e0...410e5ad3d5 = 7 commits, 0 behind: 466ade69a7 (#2625), 4dc45fe8c9 (#2521 Claude Sonnet 5.5), f600e93baf (#2484 GPT-6.2),
cd3ec48269 (Release v3.98.1, #2635), f948b5ba01 (#2119 LiteLLM+Gemini), cc271bdd98 (#2520 Ollama tasks condense), 410e5ad3d5 (#2498 OpenAI-compatible Responses).
Overlap that matters: cc271bdd98 rewrote src/api/providers/native-ollama.ts (+15/-25) and native-ollama.spec.ts (+169/-11); #2632 rewrites native-ollama.ts (+36/-45) — that is the dirty pair.
#2629 (openai-native.ts) and #2631 (bedrock.ts) also overlap files main touched, but both still report mergeable=true at 410e5ad3d5: no rebase needed there.

## HEADLINE
#2632 is NOT clear and never was in this run: mergeable=false / mergeable_state=dirty, labels awaiting-author + has-conflicts, gate pending.
queue.json already recorded it that way; the lead's group-1 brief treated all eight as clear. Seven of the eight are clear.

The one mechanism that explains every row below, read from the repo: `.coderabbit.yaml` at head 25ce2c8b45, lines 28-34 verbatim — `auto_review:` / `enabled: false` / `drafts: false` / `auto_incremental_review: true` / `auto_pause_after_reviewed_commits: 0` / `labels:` / `- "coderabbit-review-active"`. Automatic review is opt-in by that label. The reconcile workflow removes it on push and re-adds it once the required checks pass; the label event is what starts the review, 2 seconds later on average. A manual `@coderabbitai` mention is never needed after a push and does not work on an already-reviewed head.

### Hypothesis under test (labelled as a hypothesis)
Merge-commit-only pushes are not reviewable: a refresh merge whose tree equals git's auto-merge tree carries no author content, so CodeRabbit answers "No files to review" and the PR is left with no review at that head (#2489). Conflict-resolving refresh merges (#2235, #2236, #2632) carried author content and were reviewed. Positive control queued by the lead: #1523 (head 7350c58978) and #1538 (head 2ae76dc55b), both DIRTY with hand conflict resolutions — expected: second row within ~2 s of the label returning.

### Description-check rule (#2236)

### Mutant ceiling: code path on main tip `scripts/stryker-diff.mjs`
- `:11` `export const MAX_CHANGED_LINES = 500`; `:12` `export const MAX_MUTANTS = 400`.
- `:755-761` preflight: `const generatedMutants = Number(mutantMatch[1])` then `if (generatedMutants > MAX_MUTANTS) { throw new Error(`${packageEntry.id} generated ${generatedMutants} mutants in preflight (limit ${MAX_MUTANTS}). ` + "Split the PR or obtain a maintainer-reviewed narrow exclusion.") }` — it **does** throw, but inside the per-package try opened at `:746` and caught at `:806` (`advisories.push(error.message)` + row `result: "Advisory incomplete"` at `:822`), so the row stays green and the loop continues.
- Only throws made **outside** that try reach the top-level handler at `:864-866` (`console.error(`Mutation gate failed: ${error.message}`)`, `process.exitCode = 1`): the changed-line cap at `:265-270` thrown from `selectFromGit` (`:847`) before any package runs. Advisory-only ceilings: valid mutants over 400 `:713-717`, timeout ratio `:719-723`, 12-minute run `:442`; `:386` 5-minute discovery and `:402` no-related-tests throws are also caught per package.
- Side effect worth stating: a package that throws in preflight never runs, so it emits **no survivor advisories**. #1404's row printed exactly one advisory (the 560-mutant preflight line) and zero survivors; #1406's old row printed the 519 line with survivors only in types/webview files. A quiet mutation row can mean 'too big to test', not 'clean'.

### The window is rolling 60 minutes, not a clock-hour reset (decided by pairs, not assumption)
- Across-hour pairs under 60 min apart print 6: `12:08:20Z` is 49 min after `11:19:05Z` → 6 remain; `04:36:12Z` is 54 min after `03:42:03Z` → 6 remain. A clock-hour reset predicts 7 for both.
- Just-over-an-hour pairs print 7: `13:09:33Z` is 61 min after `12:08:20Z` → 7 remain; `18:34:35Z` is 62 min after `17:31:54Z` → 7 remain.
- One reading the rolling model does not fit: `08:23:28Z` printed 6 although its window contains 08:00:54Z and 08:07:00Z (predicts 5) ⇒ one of those two did not consume allowance. Mechanism for that exception is not claimed.
- Consequence recorded for queueing: space refresh pushes > 60 min apart to get a fresh 8; 49–54 min spacing costs two of eight inside one window; the printed number is the authority.
- Capture law (measured on #2661): dismissal empties the review body — `6022961343` is now DISMISSED with 0 bytes, so its allowance sentence survives only in this log — and a review round can produce a review comment (7103105120) with no review object at all. Quote allowance lines when they land.

### Read-shape facts recorded for the lanes
- REST `pulls/<n>` on this repo returns no `review_decision` key at all (keys enumerated on #1545 end at `changed_files`; there is `mergeable`, `mergeable_state`, `rebaseable`, `auto_merge`), so any 'rd <VALUE>' in a handshake is derived from review/check objects, not read from the pull.
- `pulls/<n>/reviews` with default per_page returns the **oldest 30** objects ascending — a newest-by-id read must paginate (`per_page=100` and follow pages) or sort client-side over the full set.

### Trigger law, read from main tip `050c49ef82693b02a1626a08cf8609a6f7c00223`
- `.github/workflows/mutation-testing.yml` line 4 `pull_request:`, line 5 `types: [edited, opened, reopened, ready_for_review, synchronize]`, line 7 second trigger `types: [checks_requested]`, lines 12-14 `concurrency:` / `group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.event.pull_request.number || github.ref }}` / `cancel-in-progress: true`.
- Consequence recorded: a PR body PATCH is a `pull_request` activity of a trigger type, so it re-runs this workflow on the same head at attempt 1 in a new run id; comments are free because comment activity produces no `pull_request` type. The concurrency group is per-workflow-per-PR, so an edit can only cancel an in-flight run **of that same workflow on that same PR**.

## 2026-11-23T19:0xZ — cancellation taxonomy (both shapes named by annotation[0]) and #1321 filled as a queue cell

### Standing rule adopted: for any cancelled row read `/check-runs/<id>/annotations` first and quote annotation[0]; no wall-time arithmetic
- **Shape A — runner job timeout.** #2227 `e2e-mock` row `137885470388`, started 2026-11-23T17:37:28Z, completed 18:07:45Z, conclusion cancelled, `output.annotations_count` 2. Annotation 1 (`.github:1`, annotation_level failure): `The job has exceeded the maximum execution time of 30m0s`. Annotation 2 (`.github:9449`): `The operation was canceled.`
- **Shape B — concurrency cancellation.** #1321 `mutation-diff` row `137709047451` (run `45179410341`, event pull_request, created 12:58:56Z, cancelled 12:59:11Z), annotations_count 2. Annotation 1 (`.github:1`, failure): `Canceling since a higher priority waiting request for Changed-code mutation testing-1321 exists`. Annotation 2 (`.github:56`): `The operation was canceled.` The group string inside the sentence is the repository's own concurrency key from `mutation-testing.yml` line 13 (`${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.event.pull_request.number || github.ref }}`).
- Both shapes end with the same second annotation, so `The operation was canceled.` identifies nothing on its own; annotation[0] is the discriminator.
- **Log blindness measured:** on #2227 the timeout sentence is not in the job log — `.e2e-2227-137885470388.log` (2,397,248 B) ends with `Terminate orphan process: pid (5182) (bash)` teardown lines and contains no 'exceeded' sentence — while the annotations carry it. An absence claim from stdout would have been confidently wrong here.

- Mechanism for the mismatch (pre-reviewer measured both sides and got the same 292/37): CI computes `BASE_SHA="$(git rev-parse "$HEAD_SHA^1")"`, i.e. the merge ref's first parent — the base tip GitHub used when it built the merge ref — not the pull's true merge-base, which for #1321 is `7d8053fefa1c`. A bill is therefore a measurement **of a tree against the base the runner used at that instant**; it cannot be inherited across a base move.

### What the marker fields mean (established by five points, not assumed)
- `blocking:` equals the digest's own advisory-mutant row count (82, 19, 4, 0, 0 across the five); `advisories:` counts non-mutant advisories (preflight limit line, 12-minute line, incomplete row).

In all three the mutation row still concluded **success**, and the artifacts physically contain no `extension/*` report files. **On three of the five pulls the gate measured nothing about extension** while its bill is the largest (493 / 468 / 292 lines).
- Mutants per line is not a constant: #1545 322 lines → 378 valid (1.174), #2227 428 lines → 389 valid (0.909). A 'lines crossing 400' estimate is therefore not supported by the two pulls that actually ran; the preflight limit is on mutants generated, and the next head's digest — not its line count — is the authority.

### Three-stage emission law, each stage separately evidenced
- Stage 1, the digest: uncapped survivor rows (82 / 19 / 4 / 0 / 0), preceded by the published cap text `Warning annotations highlight up to 20 unique locations (maximum 7 per file). This summary lists every advisory mutant.`
- Stage 2, the workflow's own cap: #2227 82 rows over 49 uniques in three files → **13** log warnings = 5 + 1 + 7; #1545 19 rows over 14 uniques in one file → **7** = the first seven uniques. One further log warning is added per non-mutant advisory, which is why #1406 shows 5 warnings for 4 survivor rows and #1404 shows 1 warning for 0 survivor rows.
- Stage 3, the annotation store: #2227 13 emitted → **10** returned, missing `src/core/webview/ClineProvider.ts:829`, `:842`, `:846`, order reversed. That is the only place the 'same batch, reverse order, drops the log tail' wording belongs; it explains 13→10, never 82→13 or 19→7.
- Citation rule: quote the digest row (with the marker naming the head) for what ran; quote the warning count only as the capped emission; never quote the job summary (its REST endpoint answers 404 to this token).

### Anchoring law for reconcile-run citations (my third anchoring error today)
- A run's **first stamped line is the runner boot line** 'Current runner version: 2.338.2', not a decision. Taking 'the first timestamp in the file' as the time of a decision is wrong by 3.8–6.1 s on every one of the passes measured here (5.232 s for #1406's arming, 4.865 s for #1404's, 6.050 s for #1404's strip, 3.816 s for #2227's).
- These logs also contain the workflow's own source text, so a search by label name hits lines like `desiredLabel = 'awaiting-author';` and `core.info(\`PR #${pr.number}: …\`)` — 20-plus per log — before the single real decision. The anchor must be the 28-character timestamp prefix bound to the decision text: `/^(\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT\d\d:\d\d:\d\d\.\d+Z)\s*PR #<n>:/`.
- Citation pairing rule from lead: **the duration and its anchor travel together.** Age the silence as '2 h 30 m from the decision line stamped 18:12:21.5575353Z in run 45235637138', never as a bare duration — a bare number is what someone later re-derives from a boot line and gets five seconds wrong about.
- Errors stay in the record with their sizes: 18:13:36Z / 18:21:08Z (`pull.updated_at`, +74.4 s and +5.6 s over the decisions), then 18:12:16.3255938Z / 18:20:57.5055526Z (boot lines, −5.232 s and −4.865 s), then the decision pair. The boot pair understates the wait; the updated_at pair on #1406 overstates it.
- Nothing else moved at 20:42:58Z: no CodeRabbit row on #1406 or #1404, no push on any of the five heads, no new allowance reading, #2227 and #1321 unchanged.

### Log-reading laws added the hard way
- A single reconcile run prints the same decision line twice, from two files of the same job, nine microseconds apart (20:54:53.0281108Z and 20:54:53.0281014Z in run `45264176574`): count decisions on deduplicated text.
- 'first decision line' is only meaningful as 'first inside my enumerated set of N runs over window A..B' — pre-reviewer's #1404 stripping at 18:12:17.7414094Z (run `45235627663`, a `pull_request_target` run in my 29-run set whose log I had downloaded but not scanned with the right anchor) beat my 18:12:41.7919457Z. The two **arming** firsts did not move.
- A manually dispatched pass runs in dry-run mode: workflow_dispatch run `45265018836` (created 20:59:33Z) prints 'PR #1545: stale clock dry run: true' where every triggered pass prints 'dry run: false'. Reprint fingerprint = decision lines only ('→ <label>', 'stripping state labels', 'preserving current state labels'); the 'last author activity … → none' and 'PR age … Skipping' lines vary by PR and must not be hashed in.

## 2026-11-23T23:2xZ — 'main' is three refs on one chain, and the 1 MiB that truncates a census

- Naming law, measured rather than accepted. `main` in this checkout resolves to three refs holding three different commits: `refs/remotes/upstream/main` = `050c49ef82693b02a1626a08cf8609a6f7c00223` (committed 2026-11-23T14:20:24Z), `refs/heads/main` = `d76dffa860939f79ce4657bee56364ed272d3523` (2026-11-13T05:27:09Z), `refs/remotes/origin/main` = `53cb5b5e7c8014d3226e5636bbca8999ed3b8526` (2026-11-10T23:51:47Z). `git rev-list --count` in both directions shows a single ancestry, not three forks: upstream ahead of origin by 46 with 0 back; upstream ahead of `refs/heads/main` by 40 with 0 back; `refs/heads/main` ahead of origin by 6 with 0 back. So origin/main ⊂ main ⊂ upstream/main. Every base or 'main' figure in this log now names its ref, and the CI merge base quoted from run digests is `050c49ef8269` = **refs/remotes/upstream/main**.
- Census lines reproduced: `git status --porcelain -uall` in the main worktree = 31,640 lines (measured at 23:22:40Z with a 64 MiB cap; full stdout 1,790,807 位元組), `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` = 30,140 lines — the provenance of the 30,140 figure quoted in the lane traffic. With ignored files the counts rise to 286,281 and 284,781 respectively (pre-reviewer's measurement, reported with their method).
- Instrument law from a direct probe. Node's `execFile` default `maxBuffer` is 1 MiB, which is smaller than this repo's status output, so the default truncates it. Probed at exactly 1 MiB: it raises `RangeError: stdout maxBuffer length exceeded` (`ERR_CHILD_PROCESS_STDIO_MAXBUFFER`) **and the error object carries 1,048,576 位元組 of partial stdout = 17,372 lines, with stderr 0 位元組** — the same shape as the 17,487-line near-miss pre-reviewer reported. The truncation is not silent; the silent part is catching that error and using `error.stdout` as though it were the whole census. From now on every git and gh call in my programs states `maxBuffer` explicitly, and a MAXBUFFER error invalidates the reading instead of trimming it.

Rule in force: state the cap and the API name with every large-output reading, and treat a truncation error as invalidating the reading. ### A 200 response that carries no answer, measured on my own instrument (00:5xZ)
- Deliberately bad GraphQL through a direct POST (`https://api.github.com/graphql`, JSON body, token in the Authorization header, so no shell quoting is involved at all): the query asked for `reviewDecision bogusField reviewCounts{changesRequested}` on #1406. Response: HTTP 200, body 524 位元組, `errors` an array of 2 entries — `"Field 'bogusField' doesn't exist on type 'PullRequest'"` and `"Field 'reviewCounts' doesn't exist on type 'PullRequest'"` — and the `data` key **absent from the response object entirely**, not present-and-null. The same query trimmed to `reviewDecision` alone: HTTP 200, 78 位元組, no `errors` key, `data.repository` present, `reviewDecision` `CHANGES_REQUESTED`.
- Discriminator, stated precisely enough to be coded: an HTTP 200 is not an answer. A GraphQL answer requires `data` present, non-null, and `errors` missing or empty. `data` being absent altogether is what GitHub returns when the query fails validation, so a reader that tests only `response.data` truthiness and a reader that tests only `errors.length` both work here by accident; the honest test checks both. This is why `gh pr view --json reviewCount` is a useful instrument: the CLI turns the same server error into a hard non-zero exit plus the list of valid fields, rather than a 200 with a body that looks like data.
- Field names the schema actually has, verified: `reviewDecision` exists; `bogusField` and `reviewCounts` do not. The `gh pr view` field list I tripped over earlier is a projection of this schema, so 'not a field' is a transport fact about my query, not a fact about GitHub's answer.
- Cross-instrument age arithmetic, with the rounding made explicit: pre-reviewer's census ran at 2026-11-24T00:50:40.457Z against mine at 00:47:48.752Z, a gap of 171.705 s. They reported the gap as 172.457 s, which is exactly what you get by treating my stamp as `00:47:48.000Z` — the extra 0.752 s is the rounding of my quoted stamp, not a disagreement about the object. Ages agree to the millisecond once both stamps are used raw, which is the proof that an age is a function of (object, read time). Consequence adopted: quote stamps with their milliseconds, or the offset arithmetic inherits the rounding and two correct instruments appear to disagree by a fraction of a second.
- Shape sample added to the stale-verdict finding, verified: on #2227 the newest review object `5928347858` (COMMENTED, `coderabbitai[bot]`, 2026-11-16T21:49:20Z) lands 4,041 s — 1 h 7 m 21 s — after the CHANGES_REQUESTED `5927256308` (2026-11-16T20:41:59Z), and the verdict is still CHANGES_REQUESTED at my 00:53:56Z read. Recomputed ages at that same instant: the CR is 172 h 11 m 57 s old and the newer comment-object 171 h 4 m 36 s.

### The 58-byte line, closed: what it measured, and the discard bracketed by two of my own reads
- What I actually measured at 2026-11-24T01:02:12.093Z: `git --no-optional-locks -C C:/work/Zoo-Code-fork/vps2-rb/r1545 status --porcelain -uall` exited 0 and wrote 58 位元組 total — one line, 57 位元組 of text plus one LF (` M src/api/providers/utils/__tests__/abort-signal.spec.ts`, and the line does not end in CRLF). Fixer's measurement of the file behind that line: 14,946 位元組 with 418 CRLF pairs, sha16 `808f7e498abca69f`, against a committed blob of 14,528 位元組 with 418 LF and no CR; the delta is exactly 418 位元組, one carriage return per line, with zero content change — `git diff --numstat` 418 added / 418 removed, and both `--ignore-all-space` and `--ignore-cr-at-eol` returning empty. `git diff --cached` was empty, so the file was never staged; `git check-attr -a` printed nothing, so no text/eol attribute and no automatic normalisation.
- So '1 line, 58 位元組' was the size of my command's output, and 'a 58-byte spec edit' was a category error that my own wording invited: the number described the porcelain line, not an edit. Fixer caught it by counting bytes of the output rather than accepting my framing, and the propagation path is in the record — my census line, quoted onward as an edit size, became a work item.
- My instrument failure, measured exactly: `git status --porcelain -uall --no-optional-locks` (flag after the subcommand) exits 129 with stdout 0 位元組 / 0 lines and stderr 1,248 位元組 — 26 lines by raw split, 23 by non-empty counting, the first two being `error: unknown option ` + backtick + `no-optional-locks` + backtick + ` and `usage: git status [<options>] [--] [<pathspec>...]``. Lead's 26 and my 23 are the same stderr under two counting rules, and the rule to keep is the cheap one: a non-zero exit with non-empty output is an instrument failure before it is a measurement. My own mechanism was worse than a stream merge — my helper returned the stderr text as the command's value, so the error text was counted as data by construction, which is why the fix is 'check the exit code first', not 'separate the streams'.
- Independent bracket around the discard, from my reads on the same path. Before: 01:02:12.093Z, porcelain exit 0, 1 line, 58 位元組. After: 2026-11-24T01:11:18.093Z, porcelain exit 0, 0 lines, 0 位元組; HEAD `30f60396320f…`, branch `rb-1545` unchanged; the file is 14,528 位元組, sha16 `8a0f366d4e9670de`, byte-for-byte equal to `git show HEAD:<path>`, mtime 2026-11-24T01:09:16.292Z against fixer's 01:09:16.291Z — one millisecond apart, which is the machine-copy rule working rather than a disagreement. Fixer's own bracket is tighter (BEFORE 01:09:16.262Z, ACTION 01:09:16.398Z, AFTER 01:09:16.518Z, equivalence check 01:09:16.548Z); mine is an outside confirmation that the after-state has held for two minutes and counting.

### The 1-millisecond stamp, resolved as two date paths, and the before-state gap closed by reconstruction
- Same file, same Node (v25.9.0), two outputs: `mtimeMs` as the raw float 1795482556291.7527 goes to `new Date(st.mtime).toISOString()` = 2026-11-24T01:09:16.292Z because the stat-Date path rounds to 1795482556292, while building a Date from `mtimeMs` truncated gives 1795482556291 = 01:09:16.291Z. Both are machine copies of one instant; the 1 ms is the path, not a second write. From now on every millisecond stamp I print names its path: stat-Date or reconstructed-Date. This is the third time tonight the same hazard appeared (pre-reviewer logged it once in their §59), and it is the same family as the stderr line count (26 raw split versus 23 with blanks filtered) and the 57/58 porcelain line: a number is only comparable once it carries the name of what produced it.

Fixer read the file twice at independent instants — 01:04:21.841Z and 01:09:16.262Z — both 14,946 位元組, 418 CRLF pairs, sha16 `808f7e498abca69f`, mtime 2026-10-05T13:29:09.535Z; and pre-reviewer reproduced the same bytes forward from the stored object, converting the committed blob LF→CRLF to regenerate 14,946 位元組 and `808f7e498abca69f`. ### Two refinements from fixer's addendum, both measured
- A 1-millisecond difference between the stat-Date and reconstructed-Date paths appears only when the sub-millisecond fraction carries: `mtimeMs` 1795482556291.7527 truncates to 291 and rounds to 292, so the two paths differ by 1 ms; 1795482978240.3782 truncates and rounds to the same 240, so both paths print 2026-11-24T01:16:18.240Z. The label stays on every millisecond stamp, but 'two paths, one instant' can print identically — agreement is not evidence that one of them was skipped, and a 1 ms gap is not evidence of a second write. It depends only on whether the fraction carries.
- Corroboration of a rule I applied earlier from my own experience: a parse error aborts a program before any of it executes, so a log written by appends stops exactly at the previous entry rather than half-writing the failed one. Fixer's record file proves it on their side (a failed write left the file at 190,556 位元組 / 2,474 lines / sha16 `56616370b4a826eb`, which is a strict byte prefix of the successful 194,474 位元組 / 2,520 lines / sha16 `973a89c579cd1fc2`). It matches my own two aborts tonight: the programs that died on `Expected ',', got ')'` wrote nothing, which is why re-reading state before retrying was safe.

### Same criterion, two APIs — the phrasing fix from fixer's Addendum 71
- 'We both checked the exit code' collapses two instruments into one object. My exit-code check runs through `execFile` (promisified: the rejection carries `error.code`, e.g. `ENOBUFS`/`ERR_CHILD_PROCESS_STDIO_MAXBUFFER`, plus the exit code), fixer's runs through `spawnSync` (`.status` and `.code`). One criterion, two APIs, and a quotation has to say which API produced which number — the same reason my truncation readings at 1,048,576 位元組 and pre-reviewer's at 1,052,672 位元組 were never a disagreement but two APIs at the same 1 MiB boundary, one pipe chunk apart. Fixer's citation for the record: their Addendum 71, 196,015 位元組 / 2,540 lines / sha16 `bad073c9d91c1fb3`, with the 194,474-位元組 predecessor a strict byte prefix, mtime 2026-11-24T01:17:39.782Z printed identically on both date paths because the fraction `.4165` does not carry.

### The 29-line gap closed on a byte-identical object: a filter ladder, not a repository change
- Fixer and I read the same output: `mainrepo-porcelain-20261124-0139Z.txt` is 1,819,488 位元組 with sha256 prefix `40e912d26e1f8152` on my copy, matching their digest from their 01:37:52.346Z read, and their 01:39:17.179Z-equivalent content is unchanged. Two file names differ in provenance stamp — mine carries my read time at minute granularity, theirs carried a directory mtime — and neither changes a byte of what was counted.
- The ladder on that one file (32,015 lines total), counted at 2026-11-24T01:46:33.152Z: lines containing `.dsh` at any position and any status code = **2,914** (fixer's number); containing `.dsh/` = **2,885** (my number); containing `.dsh` but not `.dsh/` = **29**, and those 29 have real names — flattened artefacts like `?? .tmp-cr-audit/u44-untracked-archive/work__Zoo-Code-fork__pr1290-wt/.dsh__ann-104620451854.json` and `.dsh__cmt-oauth-id.txt`. Untracked-only counts give the same values (2,914 and 2,885), so every `.dsh`-bearing line in the census is untracked.
- The filter swing I flagged is quantitatively confirmed on the same file: 331 lines carry quoted paths, and exactly **328** of them start `".dsh/` — which is the 2,885 − 2,557 gap between my two filters to the line. A slice-based prefix test on the raw porcelain line misses quoted paths, and the size of that miss is measurable rather than a suspicion.
- Rule, on fixer's evidence and now mine: a count that comes from a filter must be quoted with the filter — substring vs prefix, trailing slash or not, status-code restricted or not. An empty filter also produces 0, so 0 on its own proves nothing about the repository; it is a property of the expression. My `startsWith` variant would have reported 0 for the same reason if I had stripped the status column the way they did and left the separator space.

### Closed: the third instrument's expressions, my own artifact as the ±1, and the fact that our archives are not git-managed
- pre-reviewer's expressions, received 2026-11-24T01:4xZ and now on file: `spawnSync` of `git --no-optional-locks -C C:/work/Zoo-Code-fork/Zoo-Code status --porcelain -uall` with `encoding: utf8`, CRLF collapsed, empty lines dropped; untracked tested as `ln.slice(0,2) === "??"`, path `pp = ln.slice(3)`; A = `pp.indexOf(".dsh/") === 0` → 2,558, B = `pp.indexOf(".dsh/") !== -1` → 2,886, read 01:46:09.824Z over 32,016 lines. They withdrew A and kept B for the same reason I did — a quoted path never starts with `.dsh/` at index 0 after the slice — and verified `A + 328 = B` inside one run, which is the same identity I measured on my copy (331 quoted lines, 328 of them `".dsh/`).
- The ±1 is mine, and it is an observer effect rather than a repository change: I wrote `mainrepo-porcelain-20261124-0139Z.txt` (1,819,488 位元組, mtime 01:40:11.176Z) — the census output as an artifact — and because that file lives in the directory being counted, the next census includes it. Fixer's 01:37:52.346Z read and my 01:39:17.179Z read both predate the write and cannot see it; pre-reviewer's 01:46:09.824Z read and fixer's 01:50:34.030Z rescan both see it. So 2,885/2,914 and 2,886/2,915 are both correct, one second apart in effect. My own file became part of what I was counting, and saving an artifact into the counted directory is the mistake, not the saving.
- Invisibility of our archives, verified with the rule that produces it: `.gitignore` line 40 is `logs` and line 41 is `*.log` (the file itself is 62 lines and uses CRLF), so a bare `logs` swallows a directory of that name at any depth. `git check-ignore -v` answers `.gitignore:40:logs` for `.dsh/workflow/20261122-1551/logs` and `.gitignore:41:*.log` for my `watchsticky.log` and `watchqueue.log`; it exits 1 — no rule — for `gates-20261122-1551.md`, `trace1545-runs.json`, `mainrepo-porcelain-20261124-0139Z.txt` and `.comment-5557323426.md`. In the census object, lines mentioning `20261122-1551/logs/` number 0, while the directory on disk holds 725 entries at my 01:51:55.579Z read (fixer measured 724 entries / 685 regular files at 01:50:34.030Z — the same directory, one entry and a different counting unit apart: mine counts directory entries, theirs separates regular files).
- Consequence for how I cite my own evidence: an archive under `logs/` or ending in `.log` is summarisable and byte-verifiable but has no git custody — a repository census will never find it, and `git status` silence about it is not evidence of absence. My watcher logs are in exactly that class. Everything I want to be findable through the repository has to live outside those patterns, with digest and read time in the citation.

### The mechanism behind `undefined/` measured on my own instrument
- `os.tmpdir()` inside this harness returns the literal `undefined\temp`, and `process.env.TMP`, `TEMP`, `TMPDIR` and `USERPROFILE` are all unset in the same process — measured at 2026-11-24T02:10:16.914Z. So a program that resolves its temporary directory through the platform helper does not write to a scratch space at all: it writes into the working tree at `undefined/temp/`, where nothing ignores it (`check-ignore` finds no rule for `undefined/x.txt`, no rule for the directory itself, and answers `.gitignore:41:*.log` only for a `.log` file inside it), and every such write moves the root-level census. This is the mechanism pre-reviewer proposed, and it now has a second witness on a different instrument.
- Scale and age, re-measured by me at 02:08:52.127Z and by fixer at 02:09:21.653Z with identical values: 207 regular files, 6 directories, 56,474,799 位元組 (56.47 MB decimal / 53.86 MiB binary), 183 entries inside the single top-level entry `temp`, oldest birthtime 2026-11-16T17:57:46.338Z, newest mtime 2026-11-24T00:57:59.851Z. The directory is nine days old and only 52 of its files fall in my 23:22:40Z→01:37:52.346Z window, so the writer ran repeatedly, not once — fixer's point, and it changes the finding from 'a stray write' to 'a standing defect in how this harness resolves a temp path'.
- Attribution honesty: I cannot say which lane wrote which file. My own programs write to explicit `.dsh/workflow/20261122-1551/...` paths and my logs contain no reference to `undefined/temp` before tonight's discovery, which is weak evidence that I am not a contributor rather than proof; the naming shapes (`am_d49f5826f66048.json`, `an138003487421a.json` plus `.hdr` siblings, `gql_5f0f1c23ca036.json`) match annotation and GraphQL dumps that other lanes described making. Nobody should delete it on my say-so: removing it is a write of up to 207 census lines against an owner that is still unknown.

### The directory clock rebuilt on my instrument, and the seam at 02:45:43Z
- Precondition first, because without it the whole method is void: 40 pre-image copies of fixer's record on disk at 2026-11-24T03:03:21.936Z, ordered by `mtimeMs`, with heading counts and byte sizes strictly increasing across all 40 (no break) — so mtime is a faithful clock for this directory.
- Counts of copies with `mtimeMs` at or before each probe moment: 02:41:27.598Z → 31; 02:42:03.436Z → 32; 02:43:17.449Z → 32; 02:45:43.006Z → 32; 02:46:46.594Z → 34; 02:47:14.363Z → 34; 02:48:15.370Z → 34; 02:48:51.965Z → 35; 02:49:22.207Z → 36. My own two readings (32 at 02:42:03.436Z and 34 files at 02:48:15.370Z) and pre-reviewer's 32 at 02:43:17.449Z and 34 at 02:47:14.363Z all fall in window; fixer's 35 at 02:45:43Z and 36 at 02:46:46.594Z do not, where the truth is 32 and 34 — both are copies-plus-live sums that lost their population label in transit, exactly the mechanism lead filed.
- The seam, so nobody quotes a boundary without its printing path: the 33rd copy's `mtimeMs` truncates to 02:45:43.006Z while its stat-Date path prints 02:45:43.007Z, so a probe sitting exactly on 02:45:43.006Z flips between 32 and 33 depending on which printing it uses; mine used the printed value. The disputed values are two to three copies off, far outside that seam, so the verdict is unaffected — but a millisecond-precise boundary names its path, which is the anchor lesson moved one directory over.

### Three comparison paths at the seam, and the seam census across the directory
- Re-measured at 2026-11-24T03:08:52.210Z on 42 copies with all three comparison paths printed side by side. Probe 02:45:43.006Z: raw-float comparison (`mtimeMs <= t`) gives 32, floored comparison gives 33, rounded comparison gives 32. Probe 02:45:43.007Z: all three give 33. Probe 02:46:46.594Z and .595Z: all three give 34, which is why the second contested reading was never at risk — its margin is 1.503 ms and the boundary in question does not straddle it. The seam is one boundary on one copy, and only one of the three paths crosses it at one probe value.
- My own correction stands inside this: the '32 at 02:45:43.006Z' I sent to lead came from the raw-float path, which I did not name until after the fact; the same probe under the floored path reads 33. I have sent the correction, and the filter path now travels beside the read time on every count I take from a timestamped population. Population census of the hazard at 03:07:49.212Z: 15 of 41 copies carry a `mtimeMs` fractional part at or above .5, so fifteen of forty-one boundaries flip between the rounding and truncation paths — pre-reviewer's generalisation confirmed on a second instrument, and the reason fixer's rule now lists 'the rendering used on each side of every timestamp comparison' among the mandatory fields of a count.
- Fixer's two out-of-window readings stay out of window under every path: 35 and 36 against truths of 32-to-33 and 34, two to three copies away, far outside a seam that can move a count by one. Their mechanism finding — a borrowed instant, the timestamp of the object read rather than the moment of reading — is theirs to file, and it is the better diagnosis for those two rungs; my earlier population-label story was the mechanism for a different slip, and I have not tried to make it cover theirs.

### My own label slip, filed against me: two paths misnamed in a message I sent at 03:0xZ
- The program printed two columns built as `Math.floor(mtimeMs) <= t` (33) and `Math.round(mtimeMs) <= t` (32) at probe 2026-11-24T02:45:43.006Z, and my sentence described them as 'raw float versus Math.floor'. The pair I quoted was floor-versus-round; the raw-float path was a third column I had not printed in that program, and it gives 32 because a raw comparison always excludes a residue: `1795488343006.88 <= 1795488343006` is false, while flooring pulls the residue in and rounds leaves it out. Corrected wording, from the same bytes at 03:08:52.210Z: raw 32, floor 33, round 32.
- Cause and remedy, same family as the rest of the night: the labels were typed and the values were printed, so the labels had no witness. From now on the path name in my sentences is interpolated from the program that produced the number rather than written by hand beside it, which is the same prescription already applied to counts and digests — identifiers printed with their values, never recalled next to them. My six-probe set is also not pre-reviewer's six-probe set (their probes land on `.000Z`), which is why my minimum margin is 0.88 ms and theirs was 1.503 ms; a robustness claim names its set or it is not about the same thing.

### The seam ratio is a property of the arrival sequence, measured as a ladder
- Rebuilt by write order (`mtimeMs`) at 2026-11-24T03:27:21.869Z over 46 pre-images: k=40 gives 14/40 = 35.00 %, k=41 gives 15/41 = 36.59 % — a rise of 1.59 points — then 15/42 = 35.71 %, 15/43 = 34.88 %, 15/44 = 34.09 %, 15/45 = 33.33 %, 15/46 = 32.61 %. So my earlier 'it only goes down' was a plateau read as a trend: the ratio falls exactly while every arriving copy has a sub-millisecond residue below .5, and rises whenever an arriving copy crosses that line. The trigger at k=41 is the copy with `mtimeMs` 1795489510552.7114 — residue .7114, where 552 is the printed millisecond field — which is worth naming because pre-reviewer quoted the residue as .5527, reading the millisecond field as if it were the fraction. The conclusion survives the mislabel; the field does not.
- Citation rule I have taken from this: quote this ratio with its as-of denominator and numerator, name the population, and state the assumption about arrivals it is being used to support, because it is a quantity over a sequence and not an attribute of the file. My own 36.59 %, 33.33 % and 32.61 % readings are all correct at their moments, and the drift between them is explained by arrivals whose residues are all below half — measured, not assumed.

### My own precondition check failed on my own cap, and the phantom's host counted
- At 2026-11-24T03:46:36.854Z I printed the immutability precondition for the prefix ladder as false: the program read only the first 200,000 bytes of each pre-image and counted `## Addendum` headings inside that window, so at the fourth adjacent pair it compared 73 headings against 73 while byte sizes did increase (200,661 → 204,440). Re-running with full reads over the same 51 copies at 03:48:55.631Z gives strictly increasing byte sizes and heading counts with no break, last copy 370,759 位元組 with 120 headings. The cap clipped the body rather than the population, which is the 1 MiB truncation family arriving inside my own verification step: a precondition measured through a window can deny a fact the world does not deny.
- The digit string 1795491520450.5527 — the number pre-reviewer assembled from a millisecond field and a residue, which matched no field on its own object — now has exactly one host in this directory: 1 file among 51, 1 occurrence, located in its first line as the recorded `source-mtimeMs` of the state copied, in the 03-38-40-450Z archive (370,759 位元組 / 120 headings). Measured at 03:48:55.631Z. This is the dense-space claim reduced to a count, and it is why the rule is that verification runs on the named object: the string was true of something, and being true of something was never evidence of where it came from.

### The carrier census, and a stale verdict I typed over a fresh value
- Independent carrier count at 2026-11-24T04:02:41.816Z over 52 archives, entries counted in the body after stripping the header line: 65 once, 71 through 120 once each, 122 once — leaving 121 and 123 with no carrier. Extending the range to 65 (fixer's range) reconciles the two censuses onto the same six orphan rungs: 66, 67, 68, 69, 70 and 121. The archive tail shows the missing step directly: entries 119 → 120 (03-38-40-450Z-67841) → 122 (03-44-58-269Z-6f413), a step of 2 where every other step is 1, and no 03-43-43 / a7f29704 object exists on disk. Rung 121 therefore has neither a content witness nor a timestamp witness: its verdict can only ever be relayed.
- The distinction fixer asked for is operational, and my own census provides the second instance: rung 123 had no carrier at 04:02:41.816Z and gained one 1 minute 20 seconds later — the 03-59-05-502Z-842f7ed1 archive carries 123 entries — because 123 was queued for the next copy rather than skipped. A zero-carrier rung that is still zero after the next write is an orphan; one that fills within a write was waiting. That test is what separates 'the recording act missed a step' from 'not yet copied', and my board now carries a carrier column with the count of objects found carrying each rung, because asserting a recurring action obliges reporting how many objects were found carrying it.
- My own failure in this hour, filed against me: at 04:05:41.756Z my program printed watch2661's three freshest beats (elapsedMs 3,305 → 34,525 → 49,918), a due moment of 04:06:14.214Z and `overdue = false` with a margin of -32,458 ms, and then my prose called the watcher late by more than its interval and quoted the 34,525 ms pass duration from the older line. The verdict was typed from the beat I was holding in mind rather than from the table the same run printed — the same ordering fault as a verdict drafted before its count, caught here by my own printed value contradicting my own sentence. What survives is the latency finding: 2661's pass duration now dominates the 45,000 ms design sleep, so its cadence rule was switched to the measured form (expected beat = last beat + measured pass duration + design interval) and it is inside cadence.

### Where the archiving mechanism starts, computed from the directory rather than asserted
- Classifying all 54 pre-images at 2026-11-24T04:10:42.200Z by whether the filename carries an eight-hex digest segment: 3 carry no digest and hold 65, 71 and 72 entries (none of the three has a header line); 51 carry a digest and cover entries 73 through 124 continuously with exactly one gap, entry 121. So the coverage window is rung 73 onward, and inside coverage the only rung with no carrier is 121 — which is what makes it a lapse rather than a trend. Rungs 66 to 70 sit before the window: the three digest-less archives are the evidence that every-write archiving was not yet in force, so their zero carriers are 'no claim', not 'missing witness'. Fixer asked for exactly this column, and the honest version is that it came out of the naming law of the directory, not out of a chosen start.
- The header convention's start, stated with its index because the same phrase was being used two ways: 11 archives carry the `source-mtimeMs` header and the earliest of them holds 113 entries — indexed by the entry count of the state that was copied. Fixer's 'from 114' is indexed by the entry being written when the copy was taken. The two describe one event, and quoting either without naming the index is another word carrying two measurements.
- My own range lesson, bidirectional as pre-reviewer put it: the census at 04:02:41.816Z took its lower bound at 71, so it reported only rungs 121 and 123 as uncarried — rungs 66 to 70 were outside its reach rather than absent from its printout, and I reported the holes it found without the holes it never looked for. A narrow scan can assert an absence it never probed and omit an absence it did find; both bounds now print with the census.

### The 14,897-line census drop has a name: a one-mebibyte stdout cap, matched to the byte
- fixer reported a main-checkout census of 17,118 lines at 2026-11-24T04:13:17.329Z with every tracked bucket identical to mine (MM 1,157, AD 279, D 54, A 2, RD 8) and `??` down from 30,517 to 15,618, cause unnamed. My full read at 04:14:43.326Z and again at 04:16:19.897Z, with a 1 GiB buffer, gives 32,017 lines = 1,500 tracked + 30,517 untracked. Reconstructing from the same bytes: all 1,500 tracked rows plus the first 15,618 untracked rows is 17,118 lines and 1,052,672 位元組, which is the spawnSync one-mebibyte chunk shape to the byte.
- Why the shape is diagnostic rather than suggestive: `git status` prints tracked rows first and untracked rows last, so a truncated stream loses only the tail of the `??` section, which is exactly the reported pattern — tracked buckets unchanged, untracked reduced. And the reconstruction matches three independent properties of the same object at once (row count, bucket distribution, byte length), which is a much smaller coincidence space than any one of them.
- My own mislabelled derivation, filed: I first wrote that the cut landed inside row 17,119 with one byte consumed, computed from `bytes - 1`; the correct statement is that 1,052,672 位元組 is the length of the first 17,118 complete rows including the terminating newline, so the boundary coincides with a row boundary. A derived quantity that means something other than what the sentence claims is the same fault as a name mapped to the wrong algorithm, and the correction makes the identification stronger, not weaker.
- The other two-item discrepancy resolved without a mystery: my 01:39 artifact is internally consistent (32,015 lines, histogram summing to 32,015) and my current read is internally consistent (32,017 = 1,500 + 30,517); the difference of two is between two objects a quarter of an hour apart, and both added lines are mine — the evidence probe file and the census artifact itself. Every total I print now carries the components-sum-to-total boolean, computed and printed beside it.

### What my own two files cost the census, measured in bytes on matched geometry
- Comparing like with like: the current `git status --porcelain -uall` stdout including its terminating newline is 1,819,649 位元組 across 32,017 rows, and my 01:39 artifact also ends with a newline at 1,819,488 位元組 across 32,015 rows. The difference is 161 位元組, and the two added rows measure 91 and 68 位元組 of content (read row by row at 04:19:23.595Z), so 91 + 68 + 2 row separators = 161 — the delta is exactly my two files, to the byte, with nothing left over to attribute to the world.
- My own error in reaching it, filed: my first comparison stripped the trailing newline from the current output and not from the artifact, producing 160 位元組 and an arithmetic that refused to close, and my opening sentence asserted closure while the same run's own expression printed false. That is a verdict typed ahead of its value for the second time in this hour, caught by the printed boolean rather than by a reader.
- Standing consequence for my board: every census figure I quote now travels with an observer line — how many of my own files sit inside the counted tree and what they cost in rows and bytes — so my writes are subtracted arithmetically rather than explained after someone notices extra lines. The price of the rule 'keep evidence outside the tree you count' is now measured: 2 files, 2 rows, 161 位元組 on the object every lane quotes against.

### Board addendum 2026-11-24T05:22:27.483Z — the population diff is closed and the attempt question is settled
- REST became usable at the announced reset (05:15:12Z). Re-reading the same heads: #1545 via REST check-runs on 9f2848177bfb returns 34 rows (32 success, 1 skipped, 1 cancelled; component sum 34 = rows, true) and the GraphQL head-commit rollup for the same head holds 31 check runs plus one legacy status context ('Zoo Code / PR review gate', PENDING) = 32 contexts. The symmetric difference is one-directional: three rows exist only in REST — codecov/patch 138006016300 (success, completed 2026-11-23T20:53:38Z), codecov/patch/webview-patch 138006050149 (success, 20:53:39Z), CodeRabbit 138003537903 (skipped, 20:49:25Z) — and no row exists only in the rollup.
- On #2227 the two instruments coincide exactly: 21 check runs each, plus the same PENDING legacy status context in the rollup only. So the rollup is a subpopulation of the REST check-run set on these two heads and adds commit statuses that REST's check-runs endpoint does not report; row tallies should be quoted from REST with the endpoint named, and the earlier 34-versus-32 gap is superseded rather than unresolved.
- Mechanism, stated as inference with its refutation, not as measurement: the three dropped rows are all earlier-completing runs whose names reappear in the rollup with later completions (CodeRabbit 138007348847 completed 20:59:04Z; codecov rows 21:04Z), which suggests the rollup keeps one row per name-suffix per suite rather than every run. The simple form — latest run per name — is already refuted on my own data: mutation-diff appears eight times in the rollup. So the containment is measured and the cause is not.

Nobody re-ran either, so #2227's armed trigger — an attempt 2 on 137885470388 — is not met, and that sentence is now a measurement rather than a range-stated negative.
- How I nearly got that wrong, filed: my first two reads asked for a field named attempt on the check-run and run objects. That key is absent — the key is run_attempt — and gh's -q prints null for a missing key, so I held 'attempt: null' twice before checking the key list. A null produced by asking for a field that does not exist is not a finding about the field; the fix costs one call that prints the object's keys, and I am printing that key list in the record.

That is a mechanism for the morning's dead windows, and it is fixable from my side by widening the four fastest watchers' intervals; a model that assumes equal cost per pass says cutting watchhard/watchlanes/watch2661/watchsticky to a quarter of their current pass rate removes 4.00 of 7.78 passes/min (51.4%) and would project the burn near 44/min. The equal-cost assumption is the model's weak point and I name it: the watchers differ in endpoints per pass, and my site sieve undercounts two of them.

Consequence stated precisely: every rate above that uses the state endpoint pairs only rows with equal epoch, so the artifact cannot have entered (a)–(d); but it does mean that a bracket built from unmatched reads after 16:11Z is meaningless, and my own two collection attempts show it (80 attempts, 0 invalid, 78 rejected for epoch mismatch, 2 accepted — no same-epoch pair existed 160 s apart at that moment).

Counting honestly for this window: my costing calls are 3 known-costing pulls reads plus 6 charging-path probes = 9 units that I can attribute to myself; my state-endpoint attempts in this window number at least 148 across four programs (60 + 80 from the two failed collections, plus the bracket pairs and the single diagnostic probe), and whether those charged is a property of the endpoint that no instrument of mine measured here.

Projected hourly: eight throttled as now ≈ 65.2/min = 3,911.8 units/hour = 78.2% of the 5,000 shared allowance; queue-only throttled a further 4× ≈ 42.0/min = 2,518.6 units/hour = 50.4%. Recommendation: throttle watch-queue (interval 180000→720000 ms or a reduction of its per-pass endpoint fan-out), which is where the money is; keeping the current four-way throttle without touching queue is the worst of both worlds because it buys 27.2% while doubling queue's detection latency for no reason.

No real event landed in the throttled span, so no actual event-to-see latency has been measured yet — only the gap bound above.

What does not depend on the choice: the throttled period itself, 316.2822 s per pass, measured.
- Which pre-throttle interval is 'the' queue period, since pre-reviewer asked me to name it: both are measured and they answer different questions. 74.3535 s per pass is the matched pair for the 16:32:36.069Z–16:37:33.483Z span — four heartbeat intervals on the restored-but-unthrottled process, priming excluded because priming ended 16:32:36.069Z; it is the right divisor for anything whose consumption was measured inside that same span. 80.0522 s per pass is the long-window mean — 53 passes over 70.713 min from the 14:39:38.217Z spawn, including priming and every period where state changed a lot; it is the right divisor for the evening's average cadence, and it is the divisor my units-per-pass figure 41.66504770893562 was actually built with (31.22842838169063 units/min ÷ 0.7495114034153207 passes/min).
- The consequence of that pairing, stated as a limit rather than a boast: because the per-pass cost came from the long-window divisor while the new period came from the fresh four-interval measurement, the recomputed 7.904025162765654 units/min silently assumes a pass costs the same amount of quota whichever interval it sits in — that is exactly my assumption ①, and the agreement to 0.2320% is evidence about that assumption, not proof of it. pre-reviewer's own half-point is the honest ceiling: the shared bracket mean 33.96827212592358 appears in both the old projection and the new figure, so what the recomputation tested is the queue-share half of the model, and assumption ① moves from untested to 'consistent with the measured-interval conversion'. The 7.6643% spread between the two divisors (5.6987 s on 74.3535 s) also moved his zero-pass probability from 0.8633 to 0.8536, which he re-issued as a range keyed to the reference pair — the same rule, applied by him to his own number.
- One more nail in my assumption ①, from fixer, and I accept it as a limit rather than a quibble: the assumption that a pass costs the same quota no matter when it runs is the same *shape* of assumption as 'a counter tracks consumption', and that shape has already been falsified once tonight on the other budget — GraphQL moved (4,982 to 4,997 to 4,995) while fixer's lane issued no GraphQL call at all, and my core-path counters cannot see a watcher that spends on GraphQL. So ① now stands as: consistent with the measured interval conversion on the core path, unfalsified there, and structurally known to fail somewhere else in the same API. It is not promoted to proven, and the 0.2320% agreement is not allowed to promote it.
- I accept pre-reviewer's §268 decomposition and write it down as the accurate claim: the old share 7.80710709542266 units/min is identically 31.22842838169063 ÷ 4 — my rate numerator and denominator cancel, so that half contains no measured period at all; the new share 7.9040251627 units/min comes from the same numerator scaled by (60 ÷ 316.28225) ÷ 0.7495114034153207. So what actually agreed is 0.187378 passes/min (long-window rate ÷ 4) against 0.189704 passes/min (the measured period inverted): 1.2414% at the share level, diluted to 0.2320% at the total because the share is only 18.6883% of the total. Both figures share one numerator, so no second independent observation took part; the test is of the conjunction — quartering the allocation and interval-independence of per-pass cost — and the agreement cannot be attributed to either conjunct alone. My earlier sentence allowed 0.2320% to speak about assumption ①; under this reading it speaks about the conjunction, and I downgrade to that.
- Construction re-run in one program with every figure interpolated from variables (closes pre-reviewer's last layer: whether the printed formula is the formula that produced the number). Inputs are the six header readings as transcribed: remaining 3234@(b1a.t) | 3167@2026-11-24T16:28:55.399Z | 3147@2026-11-24T16:29:14.057Z | 3075@2026-11-24T16:31:15.046Z | 2979@2026-11-24T16:32:23.204Z | 2841@2026-11-24T16:34:29.284Z.

Account 1, fixed anchor with drift: edges sit at :44 plus an offset that wanders by one to two seconds, so the next edge should be about 2026-11-24T22:44:22Z or 22:44:23Z. Account 2, rolling window: the expiry is three thousand six hundred seconds after the first authenticated request that follows the previous expiry, so the next expiry is 21:44:22Z plus however long my watchers take to make their next request — with the longest measured pass interval among my watchers being 319.50375 s (queue family maximum in the interval artifact) the rolling account allows anything up to roughly 22:49:41Z. The discriminator is a single field on one read taken at a known time shortly after 21:44:22Z: if x-ratelimit-reset then reads 22:44:2x, account 1 stands and account 2 is dead; if it reads later than 22:44:25, account 2 stands and account 1 is dead. I am booking this as a scheduled read on the next board, not as a conclusion now.
